1016	  ## Development
1017	  
1018	  Prerequisites: [Node.js](https://nodejs.org/) 22+ (developed on 26) and npm.
1019	  
1020	  ```sh
1021	  npm install
1022	  npm run dev        # Vite dev server with HMR
1023	  ```
1024	  
1025	  | Script | What it does |
1026	  |---|---|
1027	  | `npm run dev` | Vite dev server |
1028	  | `npm run build` | Typecheck (`tsc -b`) + production build to `dist/` |
1029	  | `npm test` | Vitest, single run (`npm run test:watch` for watch mode) |
1030	  | `npm run lint` | [Oxlint](https://oxc.rs/docs/guide/usage/linter.html) |
1031	  | `npm run format` | Prettier, write mode (`format:check` in CI) |
1032	  | `npm run typecheck` | `tsc -b` only |
1033	  | `npm run preview` | Serve the production build via Vite |
1034	  | `npx wrangler dev` | Serve the production build the way Cloudflare Workers will (build first) |
1035	  | `npm run deploy` | Build and deploy to Cloudflare Workers (manual escape hatch; CI deploys `main` automatically) |
1036	  
1037	  ### Architecture in one paragraph
1038	  
1039	  Analysis runs entirely in the browser: a Web Worker
1040	  (`src/worker/analysis.worker.ts`) streams the uploaded JSONL through the
1041	  engine (`src/engine/` — pure TypeScript, no DOM, unit-testable in Node) and
1042	  posts progress/results back over a typed message protocol. The frozen
1043	  engine contract lives in `src/engine/contract.ts` (with `pricing.ts` and
1044	  `protocol.ts`) — read its header before touching engine code; changes
1045	  require touching [docs/PLAN.md](docs/PLAN.md). The current engine is the
1046	  WP-02 stub returning canned data.
1047	  
1048	  ### Debug logging
1049	  
1050	  All logging goes through `src/lib/logger.ts` (see docs/PLAN.md, decision
1051	  D13): console-only, never transmitted anywhere, quiet (`warn`) by default in
1052	  production builds. To get verbose output for troubleshooting, either:
1053	  
1054	  - add `?debug=1` to the URL, or
1055	  - run `localStorage.setItem('cta-debug', '1')` in the devtools console (and
1056	    reload; remove with `localStorage.removeItem('cta-debug')`).
1057	  
1058	  When contributing: never log session-log-derived strings (titles, paths,
1059	  branches, prompts) or file contents — counts, enums, durations, and error
1060	  codes only.
1061	  
1062	  ## How changes ship
1063	  v26.8.1

1069	    /**
1070	     * WP-03 — byte stream -> JSONL lines, with the per-line length cap.
1071	     *
1072	     * The parser must never hold the whole file (or one runaway line) in
1073	     * memory (docs/PLAN.md §2, "Resource limits"). This splitter keeps only the
1074	     * bytes of the line currently being assembled; once a line exceeds
1075	     * `MAX_LINE_LENGTH_BYTES` its pending bytes are dropped and the rest of the
1076	     * line is discarded as it streams past, so peak memory is bounded by the
1077	     * cap plus one input chunk regardless of file or line size.
1078	     *
1079	     * Lines are split on `\n` (an ASCII byte, so splitting before UTF-8
1080	     * decoding is safe); a trailing `\r` and a leading BOM are removed.
1081	     */
1082	    
1083	    import { MAX_LINE_LENGTH_BYTES } from './contract'
1084	    
1085	    const NEWLINE = 0x0a
1086	    const CARRIAGE_RETURN = 0x0d
1087	    
1088	    export type LineEvent =
1089	      | { kind: 'line'; text: string }
1090	      /** A line longer than the cap; its bytes were never assembled. */
1091	      | { kind: 'capped'; bytes: number }
1092	    
1093	    export class JsonlLineSplitter {
1094	      private pending: Uint8Array[] = []
1095	      private pendingBytes = 0
1096	      /** True while skipping the remainder of an over-cap line. */
1097	      private discarding = false
1098	      private discardedBytes = 0
1099	      private readonly decoder = new TextDecoder('utf-8')
1100	      private sawFirstLine = false
1101	      /** Diagnostics for the memory-bound tests: most bytes ever buffered. */
1102	      peakPendingBytes = 0
1103	    
1104	      constructor(private readonly maxLineBytes: number = MAX_LINE_LENGTH_BYTES) {}
1105	    
1106	      /** Feed one chunk; returns the complete lines it terminated, in order. */
1107	      push(chunk: Uint8Array): LineEvent[] {
1108	        const events: LineEvent[] = []
1109	        let start = 0
1110	        for (let i = 0; i < chunk.length; i++) {
1111	          if (chunk[i] !== NEWLINE) continue
1112	          this.append(chunk.subarray(start, i))
1113	          events.push(this.flushLine())
1114	          start = i + 1
1115	        }
1116	        if (start < chunk.length) this.append(chunk.subarray(start))
1117	        return events
1118	      }
1119	    
1120	      /** Signal end of input; returns the final unterminated line, if any. */
1121	      finish(): LineEvent[] {
1122	        if (this.discarding) return [this.flushLine()]
1123	        if (this.pendingBytes === 0) return []
1124	        return [this.flushLine()]
1125	      }
1126	    
1127	      private append(segment: Uint8Array): void {
1128	        if (segment.length === 0) return
1129	        if (this.discarding) {
1130	          this.discardedBytes += segment.length
1131	          return
1132	        }
1133	        if (this.pendingBytes + segment.length > this.maxLineBytes) {
1134	          // Over the cap: drop what we have and skip to the next newline.
1135	          this.discardedBytes = this.pendingBytes + segment.length
1136	          this.pending = []
1137	          this.pendingBytes = 0
1138	          this.discarding = true
1139	          return
1140	        }
1141	        // Copy: the caller may reuse the chunk's backing buffer.
1142	        this.pending.push(segment.slice())
1143	        this.pendingBytes += segment.length
1144	        if (this.pendingBytes > this.peakPendingBytes) this.peakPendingBytes = this.pendingBytes
1145	      }
1146	    
1147	      private flushLine(): LineEvent {
1148	        if (this.discarding) {
1149	          const bytes = this.discardedBytes
1150	          this.discarding = false
1151	          this.discardedBytes = 0
1152	          this.sawFirstLine = true
1153	          return { kind: 'capped', bytes }
1154	        }
1155	        let bytes: Uint8Array
1156	        if (this.pending.length === 1) {
1157	          bytes = this.pending[0]
1158	        } else {
1159	          bytes = new Uint8Array(this.pendingBytes)
1160	          let offset = 0
1161	          for (const part of this.pending) {
1162	            bytes.set(part, offset)
1163	            offset += part.length
1164	          }
1165	        }
1166	        this.pending = []
1167	        this.pendingBytes = 0
1168	        if (bytes.length > 0 && bytes[bytes.length - 1] === CARRIAGE_RETURN) {
1169	          bytes = bytes.subarray(0, bytes.length - 1)
1170	        }
1171	        // Non-streaming decode: each line is complete, so multi-byte sequences
1172	        // never straddle calls. The decoder strips a leading BOM by default,
1173	        // which is what we want on the first line only.
1174	        let text = this.decoder.decode(bytes)
1175	        if (this.sawFirstLine && bytes.length >= 3 && bytes[0] === 0xef && bytes[1] === 0xbb) {
1176	          // Preserve a BOM that appears mid-file: it is data, not a fil
1177	    ... [1055 characters truncated]

1224	[tool use: Write]
1225	  file_path: ~/Code/cache-ttl-analyzer/src/engine/parser.ts
1226	  content:
1227	    /**
1228	     * WP-03 — streaming JSONL session parser producing `ParsedSession`.
1229	     *
1230	     * Implements the docs/PLAN.md §2 correctness rules that belong to parsing
1231	     * (dedup on `message.id`, `<synthetic>` exclusion, skip-and-count, version
1232	     * warnings, request-start pairing, thread keys) and the validation /
1233	     * security rules (three verdicts, numeric hygiene, typed-record copying,
1234	     * line-length cap). It reads a dozen metadata fields and NEVER
1235	     * `message.content` — `parser.test.ts` proves that with a content-poison
1236	     * session and a field-access audit.
1237	     *
1238	     * Two layers:
1239	     *  - `SessionParser` is line-fed (`feedLine` / `feedCappedLine` / `finish`)
1240	     *    and synchronous, so unit tests drive it directly.
1241	     *  - `parseSession` wraps it around a `ReadableStream` via
1242	     *    `jsonl-stream.ts`, adding progress and cancellation for the worker.
1243	     *
1244	     * Nothing here spreads or retains a parsed object: every field is copied
1245	     * out through a typed accessor and the object is dropped, which also
1246	     * neutralizes prototype-pollution keys (`__proto__`, `constructor`).
1247	     */
1248	    
1249	    import {
1250	      MALFORMED_LINE_REJECT_RATIO,
1251	      MAX_METADATA_STRING_LENGTH,
1252	      VALIDATED_VERSION_RANGE,
1253	      type EngineProgress,
1254	      type ParsedSession,
1255	      type ParseStats,
1256	      type ParseWarning,
1257	      type RequestRecord,
1258	      type RequestUsage,
1259	      type SessionMetadata,
1260	      type ValidationVerdict,
1261	    } from './contract'
1262	    import { readJsonlLines, type LineEvent } from './jsonl-stream'
1263	    
1264	    /** `RequestRecord.threadId` for the main conversation (contract). */
1265	    export const MAIN_THREAD_ID = 'main'
1266	    /** Prefix for threads recovered from legacy interleaved sidechains (contract). */
1267	    export const LEGACY_SIDECHAIN_THREAD_PREFIX = 'sidechain-'
1268	    /** The model id Claude Code writes on API-error placeholder rows (§6.2). */
1269	    export const SYNTHETIC_MODEL_ID = '<synthetic>'
1270	    /** Default for absent/null `service_tier` and `speed` (F5). */
1271	    export const DEFAULT_SERVICE_TIER = 'standard'
1272	    export const DEFAULT_SPEED = 'standard'
1273	    /**
1274	     * A hostile file can invent unlimited record types; past this many distinct
1275	     * ones the rest aggregate under `OTHER_SKIPPED_TYPES_KEY`.
1276	     */
1277	    export const MAX_DISTINCT_SKIPPED_TYPES = 100
1278	    export const OTHER_SKIPPED_TYPES_KEY = '<other>'
1279	    /** ISO-8601 with milliseconds is 24 chars; anything longer is not a timestamp. */
1280	    export const MAX_TIMESTAMP_LENGTH = 64
1281	    /** Progress is reported at most once per this many bytes. */
1282	    export const PROGRESS_INTERVAL_BYTES = 1024 * 1024

1472	    const encoder = new TextEncoder()
1473	    
1474	    function collect(splitter: JsonlLineSplitter, chunks: (string | Uint8Array)[]): LineEvent[] {
1475	      const events: LineEvent[] = []
1476	      for (const chunk of chunks) {
1477	        const bytes = typeof chunk === 'string' ? encoder.encode(chunk) : chunk
1478	        events.push(...splitter.push(bytes))
1479	      }
1480	      events.push(...splitter.finish())
1481	      return events
1482	    }
1483	    
1484	    function texts(events: LineEvent[]): string[] {
1485	      return events.map((e) => (e.kind === 'line' ? e.text : `<capped ${e.bytes}>`))
1486	    }
1487	    
1488	    /** A stream of `count` chunks, each produced lazily by `make`. */
1489	    function chunkStream(count: number, make: (i: number) => Uint8Array): ReadableStream<Uint8Array> {
1490	      let i = 0
1491	      return new ReadableStream<Uint8Array>({
1492	        pull(controller) {
1493	          if (i >= count) {
1494	            controller.close()
1495	            return
1496	          }
1497	          controller.enqueue(make(i++))
1498	        },
1499	      })
1500	    }

1660	      const manifestPath = manifestPathFor(path)

1759	    const CHUNK_BYTES = 256 * 1024
1760	    
1761	    describe('parseSession on a 100MB synthetic file', () => {
1762	      it(
1763	        'streams it in bounded memory and gets every count right',
1764	        async () => {
1765	          const manifest = ensureLargeFixture()
1766	          expect(manifest.bytes).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(LARGE_FIXTURE_TARGET_BYTES)
1767	    
1768	          const splitter = new JsonlLineSplitter()
1769	          const progress: EngineProgress[] = []
1770	          const stream = Readable.toWeb(
1771	            createReadStream(LARGE_FIXTURE_PATH, { highWaterMark: CHUNK_BYTES }),
1772	          ) as ReadableStream<Uint8Array>
1773	    
1774	          const parsed = await parseSession(stream, {
1775	            fileName: 'large-session.jsonl',
1776	            fileSizeBytes: manifest.bytes,
1777	            splitter,
1778	            onProgress: (p) => progress.push(p),
1779	          })
1780	    
1781	          // Memory: the splitter only ever buffers one partial line plus the
1782	          // chunk that completes it — nowhere near the file or even the cap.
1783	          expect(splitter.peakPendingBytes).toBeLessThan(MAX_LINE_LENGTH_BYTES)
1784	          expect(splitter.peakPendingBytes).toBeLessThan(CHUNK_BYTES)
1785	    
1786	          expect(parsed.stats).toEqual({
1787	            totalLines: manifest.totalLines,
1788	            nonEmptyLines: manifest.totalLines,
1789	            malformedLines: 0,
1790	            skippedRecordTypes: manifest.skippedRecordTypes,
1791	            assistantRows: manifest.assistantRows,
1792	            dedupedRequests: manifest.requests,
1793	            syntheticRowsExcluded: 0,
1794	            invalidUsageRowsSkipped: 0,
1795	          })
1796	          expect(parsed.requests).toHaveLength(manifest.requests)
1797	          expect(parsed.verdict).toBe('valid-with-warnings')
1798	          expect(parsed.warnings).toEqual([
1799	            { kind: 'skipped-record-types', types: manifest.skippedRecordTypes },
1800	          ])
1801	          expect(parsed.metadata.title).toBe(manifest.lastTitle)
1802	          expect(parsed.metadata.models).toEqual(['claude-fable-5'])
1803	          expect(parsed.metadata.fileSizeBytes).toBe(manifest.bytes)
1804	    
1805	          // Every request resolved its start from a user ancestor, in order.
1806	          expect(parsed.requests.every((r) => r.requestStartSource === 'user-ancestor')).toBe(true)
1807	          expect(parsed.requests.every((r) => r.threadId === MAIN_THREAD_ID)).toBe(true)
1808	          expect(parsed.requests[0].messageId).toBe('msg_large_0')
1809	          expect(parsed.requests.at(-1)?.messageId).toBe(`msg_large_${manifest.requests - 1}`)
1810	    
1811	          // Progress: monotonic, roughly once per MB, ending at the exact size.
1812	          expect(progress.length).toBeGreaterThan(50)
1813	          for (let i = 1; i < progress.length; i++) {
1814	            expect(progress[i].bytesProcessed).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(progress[i - 1].bytesProcessed)
1815	          }
1816	          expect(progress.at(-1)).toEqual({
1817	            phase: 'parsing',
1818	            bytesProcessed: manifest.bytes,
1819	            totalBytes: manifest.bytes,
1820	            requestsSeen: manifest.requests,
1821	          })
1822	        },
1823	        120_000,
1824	      )
1825	    })

3061	## Common Pitfalls
3062	
3063	- Don't truncate inputs when passing files or content to the API. If the content is too long to fit in the context window, notify the user and discuss options (chunking, summarization, etc.) rather than silently truncating.
3064	- **Prefill removed (Fable 5, Opus 5, Sonnet 5, and the 4.6/4.7/4.8 family):** Assistant message prefills (last-assistant-turn prefills) return a 400 error on Fable 5, Opus 5, Sonnet 5, Opus 4.6, Opus 4.7, Opus 4.8, and Sonnet 4.6. Use structured outputs (`output_config.format`) or system prompt instructions to control response format instead. (One exception: the fallback-credit prefill claim - when redeeming a credit with `fallback_has_prefill_claim: true`, the server accepts the echoed assistant message; see the migration guide's refusal section.)
3065	- **Confirm migration scope before editing:** When a user asks to migrate code to a newer Claude model without naming a specific file, directory, or file list, **ask which scope to apply first** - the entire working directory, a specific subdirectory, or a specific set of files. Do not start editing until the user confirms. Imperative phrasings like "migrate my codebase", "move my project to X", "upgrade to Sonnet 4.6", or bare "migrate to Opus 4.8" are **still ambiguous** - they tell you what to do but not where, so ask. Proceed without asking only when the prompt names an exact file, a specific directory, or an explicit file list ("migrate `app.py`", "migrate everything under `services/`", "update `a.py` and `b.py`"). See `shared/model-migration.md` Step 0.
3066	- **`max_tokens` defaults:** Don't lowball `max_tokens` - hitting the cap truncates output mid-thought and requires a retry. For non-streaming requests, default to `~16000` (keeps responses under SDK HTTP timeouts). For streaming requests, default to `~64000` (timeouts aren't a concern, so give the model room). Only go lower when you have a hard reason: classification (`~256`), cost caps, deliberately short outputs, or **`max_tokens: 0`** for cache pre-warming (see `shared/prompt-caching.md` -> Pre-warming).
3067	- **Disabling thinking on Claude Opus 5 has two failure modes - prefer low/medium effort instead.** Only affects code that explicitly opts out; thinking is on by default, so watch for a disabled-thinking setting carried forward from Opus 4.8. With `thinking: {type: "disabled"}`, the model occasionally writes a tool call into its **visible text** instead of a `tool_use` block: the turn succeeds, the call never runs, no error is raised, and in an agentic loop that text pollutes later turns. It can also leak `<thinking>` tags into the response. Turning thinking on and lowering `effort` fixes both and still cuts cost. If a route must stay thinking-off: **delete** any don't-think/don't-reason rule (it makes tag leakage worse), don't name thinking tags, and add the combined instruction *"When you use a tool, you may say a brief sentence first. If no tool can express what the user asked for, say so instead of guessing. Do not include internal or system XML tags in your response."* Details: `shared/model-migration.md` -> Two failure modes when thinking is disabled.
3068	- **128K output tokens:** Fable 5, Opus 5, Opus 4.6, Opus 4.7, Opus 4.8, Sonnet 5, and Sonnet 4.6 support up to 128K `max_tokens`, but the SDKs require streaming for values that large to avoid HTTP timeouts. Use `.stream()` with `.get_final_message()` / `.finalMessage()`.
3069	- **Tool call JSON parsing (Fable 5, Opus 5, and the 4.6/4.7/4.8 family):** Fable 5, Opus 5, Opus 4.6, Opus 4.7, Opus 4.8, and Sonnet 4.6 may produce different JSON string escaping in tool call `input` fields (e.g., Unicode or forward-slash escaping). Always parse tool inputs with `json.loads()` / `JSON.parse()` - never do raw string matching on the serialized input.
3070	- **Structured outputs (all models):** Use `output_config: {format: {...}}` instead of the deprecated `output_format` parameter on `messages.create()`. This is a general API change, not 4.6-specific.
3071	- **Don't reimplement SDK functionality:** The SDK provides high-level helpers - use them instead of building from scratch. Specifically: use `stream.finalMessage()` instead of wrapping `.on()` events in `new Promise()`; use typed exception classes (`Anthropic.RateLimitError`, etc.) instead of string-matching error messages; use SDK types (`Anthropic.MessageParam`, `Anthropic.Tool`, `Anthropic.Message`, etc.) instead of redefining equivalent interfaces.
3072	- **Error handling - catch a chain, not one broad class.** A single `except APIStatusError` / `catch (AnthropicServiceException)` / `rescue APIError` loses the distinction between retryable (429, >=500, network) and non-retryable (400/404) failures. Write a most-specific-first chain - e.g. `NotFoundError` -> `RateLimitError` -> `APIStatusError` -> `APIConnectionError` (or the Go equivalent: `errors.As` into `*anthropic.Error` then `switch apierr.StatusCode { case 404: ...; case 429: ...; default: ... }`). Per-language class names and namespaces are in `shared/error-codes.md`.
3073	- **Don't research SDK types - write first.** If a type name isn't shown in the documentation included in this skill, write the code file from the namespace/package tables in the language-specific doc and let the compiler's error point you to the right name. Do not spend turns on WebFetch, SDK-repo clones, or compiling-and-running a separate reflection program to discover type names before writing - produce the source file first, then fix what the compiler reports. A quick `strings` / `jar tf` / `javap` against the installed SDK is acceptable for locating names (it returns in seconds), but don't escalate beyond that. A file with a wrong type name is recoverable; a session spent on discovery with no file written is not.
3074	- **Bash and text editor tools are Anthropic-defined, schema-less.** Declare `{"type": "bash_20250124", "name": "bash"}` / `{"type": "text_editor_20250728", "name": "str_replace_based_edit_tool"}` - no `input_schema`. A custom tool with your own schema named `"bash"` is a different tool. Handler paths and security checks are in `shared/tool-use-concepts.md` § Client-Side Tools.
3075	- **Advisor tool model pairing.** The advisor tool's `model` must be at least as capable as the request's top-level `model` - e.g. executor `claude-sonnet-5` -> advisor `claude-opus-4-8` or `claude-opus-4-7`. An invalid pair returns 400. Pairing table in `shared/tool-use-concepts.md` § Advisor. Availability: `shared/platform-availability.md`.
3076	- **Agent Skills != Managed Agents.** To have Claude generate a `.pptx`/`.xlsx`/etc. via Agent Skills, call `client.beta.messages.create` with `container={"skills": [...]}`, the `code_execution_20260521` tool, and the `code-execution-2025-08-25` beta (Skills is out of beta - no `skills-2025-10-02` header needed). Do not use `client.beta.agents` / `sessions` / `environments` here - those are the Managed Agents surface, not Agent Skills.
3077	- **MCP connector needs both halves.** `mcp_servers=[{type:"url", url, name}]` alone is rejected as a validation error - also add `tools=[{type:"mcp_toolset", mcp_server_name:<same name>}]` with beta `mcp-client-2025-11-20`. Availability: `shared/platform-availability.md`.
3078	- **`inference_geo` is a direct top-level request parameter** - `client.messages.create(..., inference_geo="us")` / `.inferenceGeo("us")`. Do not put it in `extra_body` / `putAdditionalBodyProperty`. (Messages API only - on Managed Agents, `inference_geo` instead nests inside the agent's `model` object, never top-level; see `shared/managed-agents-core.md` § Pinning inference geography.) Supported on Opus 4.6 / Sonnet 4.6 and later; availability: `shared/platform-availability.md`. `response.usage.inference_geo` reports where inference ran.
3079	- **Fine-grained tool streaming is not a beta feature.** Set `eager_input_streaming: true` on the tool definition and call the regular `client.messages.stream(...)`. There is no beta header and no `client.beta.*` path.
3080	- **Cache diagnostics is beta.** Use `client.beta.messages.*` with beta `cache-diagnosis-2026-04-07`. Pass `diagnostics: {previous_message_id: null}` on the first turn and `diagnostics: {previous_message_id: <previous response id>}` on subsequent turns; the result is on `response.diagnostics`. Availability: `shared/platform-availability.md`.
3081	- **Memory tool type is `memory_20250818`.** Declare `{"type": "memory_20250818", "name": "memory"}`. Go uses the beta-namespace type `{OfMemoryTool20250818: &anthropic.BetaMemoryTool20250818Param{}}` on `client.Beta.Messages.New`; Python/TypeScript/Ruby/PHP/C# use the non-beta `client.messages.create`; Java has both a non-beta `MemoryTool20250818` and a beta tool-runner path. Python/TypeScript provide `BetaAbstractMemoryTool` / `betaMemoryTool` helpers for implementing the backend.
3082	- **Use a model the feature actually supports.** Some features are restricted to specific model tiers - fast mode is Claude Opus 5 / Opus 4.8 only (and Claude API only), task budgets (Messages API only - Managed Agents session budgets have no model-tier restriction) are Claude Opus 5 / Fable 5 / Sonnet 5 / Opus 4.8 / 4.7 only, and the advisor tool requires a valid executor<->advisor pair. If the user's prompt names a model that the feature doesn't support, use a supported model instead and note the substitution in the output.
3083	- **Don't define custom types for SDK data structures:** The SDK exports types for all API objects. Use `Anthropic.MessageParam` for messages, `Anthropic.Tool` for tool definitions, `Anthropic.ToolUseBlock` / `Anthropic.ToolResultBlockParam` for tool results, `Anthropic.Message` for responses. Defining your own `interface ChatMessage { role: string; content: unknown }` duplicates what the SDK already provides and loses type safety.
3084	- **Report and document output:** For tasks that produce reports, documents, or visualizations, the code execution sandbox has `python-docx`, `python-pptx`, `matplotlib`, `pillow`, and `pypdf` pre-installed. Claude can generate formatted files (DOCX, PDF, charts) and return them via the Files API - consider this for "report" or "document" type requests instead of plain stdout text.
3085	- **Server-tool errors don't raise.** Web search and web fetch errors return HTTP 200 with a `web_search_tool_result` / `web_fetch_tool_result` block whose `content` is a single error object (e.g. `{error_code: "max_uses_exceeded"}`) - not a raised exception. For web search, a success `content` is a *list*; an error `content` is an *object* - branch on that before indexing.
3086	- **Managed Agents web tools ignore the environment's `networking`.** `web_search` / `web_fetch` run on Anthropic's servers in cloud *and* self-hosted environments, and Console org-level web settings apply to the Messages API only. Restrict them per tool with `allowed_domains` **or** `blocked_domains` (never both; 1-64 plain hostnames per list, subdomains covered; IPs, bare TLDs, single-label and `localhost`-style names rejected on both tools; a path suffix is allowed only on `web_search`) on the toolset `configs` entry - `shared/managed-agents-tools.md` § Web search & web fetch settings.
3087	- **Code execution output block type:** `code_execution_20260521` returns `bash_code_execution_tool_result` (with `.content.stdout`), **not** the legacy bare `code_execution_tool_result`. Iterate `response.content` and match on the correct type.
3088	- **Tool search: never defer everything.** The search tool itself must not have `defer_loading: true`, and at least one tool in `tools` must be non-deferred, or the API returns 400 `All tools have defer_loading set`.
3089	
3090	## Detected Language: typescript

3094	<doc path="typescript/claude-api/README.md">
3095	# Claude API - TypeScript
3096	
3097	| Feature | Namespace | Key types / call |
3098	|---|---|---|
3099	| User profiles | beta | `client.beta.userProfiles.create(...)` / `.retrieve(id)` / `.list()`. Pass the returned profile id on `client.beta.messages.create`. Requires a beta header - check the SDK's beta-headers reference for the current flag. |

3101	## Installation
3102	
3103	```bash
3104	npm install @anthropic-ai/sdk
3105	```
3106	
3107	> **Reading local files (ESM):** `__dirname` and `__filename` are **undefined** in ES modules - using either throws `ReferenceError: __dirname is not defined` at runtime. For cwd-relative reads, pass the bare relative path (`fs.readFileSync("./sample.png")`). For script-relative paths, derive the directory from `import.meta.url`: `const here = path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url))`. Never write `path.join(__dirname, ...)` in an ESM `.ts` file.
3108	
3109	## Client Initialization
3110	
3111	```typescript
3112	import Anthropic from "@anthropic-ai/sdk";
3113	
3114	// Default - resolves credentials from the environment:
3115	// ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, or ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN, or an `ant auth login` profile.
3116	// Prefer this for local dev; don't hardcode a key.
3117	const client = new Anthropic();
3118	
3119	// Explicit API key (only when you must inject a specific key)
3120	const client = new Anthropic({ apiKey: "your-api-key" });
3121	```
3122

3124	
3125	## Basic Message Request
3126	
3127	```typescript
3128	const response = await client.messages.create({
3129	  model: "claude-opus-5",
3130	  max_tokens: 16000,
3131	  messages: [{ role: "user", content: "What is the capital of France?" }],
3132	});
3133	// response.content is ContentBlock[] - a discriminated union. Narrow by .type
3134	// before accessing .text (TypeScript will error on content[0].text without this).
3135	for (const block of response.content) {
3136	  if (block.type === "text") {
3137	    console.log(block.text);
3138	  }
3139	}
3140	```
3141	
3142	---
3143	
3144	## System Prompts
3145	
3146	```typescript
3147	const response = await client.messages.create({
3148	  model: "claude-opus-5",
3149	  max_tokens: 16000,
3150	  system:
3151	    "You are a helpful coding assistant. Always provide examples in Python.",
3152	  messages: [{ role: "user", content: "How do I read a JSON file?" }],
3153	});
3154	```
3155	
3156	### Mid-conversation system messages (model-gated)
3157	
3158	For operator instructions that arrive mid-conversation (mode switches, injected state), append `{role: "system", ...}` to `messages` instead of editing top-level `system` - this preserves the cached prefix and carries operator authority. Must follow a user message (or an `assistant` message ending in server-tool use), and must be either the last entry in `messages` or be followed by an `assistant` turn; cannot be `messages[0]`. Unsupported models return a 400 (`role 'system' is not supported on this model`). See `shared/prompt-caching.md` for when to use this vs. top-level `system`.
3159	
3160	```typescript
3161	// No beta header needed - use regular client.messages.create.
3162	const response = await client.messages.create({
3163	  model: MODEL_ID, // must support mid-conversation system messages
3164	  max_tokens: 16000,
3165	  system: [
3166	    { type: "text", text: STABLE_SYSTEM, cache_control: { type: "ephemeral" } },
3167	  ],
3168	  messages: [
3169	    ...history,
3170	    { role: "user", content: userMessage },
3171	    { role: "system", content: "Terse mode enabled - keep responses under 40 words." },
3172	  ],
3173	});
3174	```

3227	
3228	## Prompt Caching
3229	
3230	**Caching is a prefix match** - any byte change anywhere in the prefix invalidates everything after it. For placement patterns, architectural guidance (frozen system prompt, deterministic tool order, where to put volatile content), and the silent-invalidator audit checklist, read `shared/prompt-caching.md`.
3231	
3232	### Automatic Caching (Recommended)
3233	
3234	Use top-level `cache_control` to automatically cache the last cacheable block in the request:
3235	
3236	```typescript
3237	const response = await client.messages.create({
3238	  model: "claude-opus-5",
3239	  max_tokens: 16000,
3240	  cache_control: { type: "ephemeral" }, // auto-caches the last cacheable block
3241	  system: "You are an expert on this large document...",
3242	  messages: [{ role: "user", content: "Summarize the key points" }],
3243	});
3244	```
3245	
3246	### Manual Cache Control
3247	
3248	For fine-grained control, add `cache_control` to specific content blocks:
3249	
3250	```typescript
3251	const response = await client.messages.create({
3252	  model: "claude-opus-5",
3253	  max_tokens: 16000,
3254	  system: [
3255	    {
3256	      type: "text",
3257	      text: "You are an expert on this large document...",
3258	      cache_control: { type: "ephemeral" }, // default TTL is 5 minutes
3259	    },
3260	  ],
3261	  messages: [{ role: "user", content: "Summarize the key points" }],
3262	});
3263	
3264	// With explicit TTL (time-to-live)
3265	const response2 = await client.messages.create({
3266	  model: "claude-opus-5",
3267	  max_tokens: 16000,
3268	  system: [
3269	    {
3270	      type: "text",
3271	      text: "You are an expert on this large document...",
3272	      cache_control: { type: "ephemeral", ttl: "1h" }, // 1 hour TTL
3273	    },
3274	  ],
3275	  messages: [{ role: "user", content: "Summarize the key points" }],
3276	});
3277	```
3278	
3279	### Verifying Cache Hits
3280	
3281	```typescript
3282	console.log(response.usage.cache_creation_input_tokens); // tokens written to cache (~1.25x cost)
3283	console.log(response.usage.cache_read_input_tokens);     // tokens served from cache (~0.1x cost)
3284	console.log(response.usage.input_tokens);                // uncached tokens (full cost)
3285	```
3286	
3287	If `cache_read_input_tokens` is zero across repeated identical-prefix requests, a silent invalidator is at work - `Date.now()` or a UUID in the system prompt, non-deterministic key ordering, or a varying tool set. See `shared/prompt-caching.md` for the full audit table.
3288	
3289	---
3290	
3291	## Extended Thinking
3292	
3293	> **Fable 5, Claude Opus 5, Opus 4.8, Opus 4.7, Opus 4.6, and Sonnet 4.6:** Use adaptive thinking. `budget_tokens` is removed on Fable 5, Claude Opus 5, Opus 4.8, and 4.7 (400 if sent); deprecated on Opus 4.6 and Sonnet 4.6.
3294	> **Claude Opus 5:** thinking is on by default - omitting `thinking` runs adaptive (`{ type: "adaptive" }` is equivalent), unlike Opus 4.8/4.7 where omitting it meant no thinking. `{ type: "disabled" }` is accepted only at effort `high` or lower; pairing it with `xhigh`/`max` returns a 400.
3295	> **Older models:** Use `thinking: {type: "enabled", budget_tokens: N}` (must be < `max_tokens`, min 1024).
3296	
3297	```typescript
3298	// Fable 5 / Claude Opus 5 / Opus 4.8 / 4.7 / 4.6: adaptive thinking (recommended)
3299	const response = await client.messages.create({
3300	  model: "claude-opus-5",
3301	  max_tokens: 16000,
3302	  thinking: { type: "adaptive", display: "summarized" }, // display opt-in: default is omitted (empty thinking text) on Fable 5 / Mythos 5 / Claude Opus 5 / Opus 4.8 / 4.7
3303	  output_config: { effort: "high" }, // low | medium | high | xhigh | max
3304	  messages: [
3305	    { role: "user", content: "Solve this math problem step by step..." },
3306	  ],
3307	});
3308	
3309	for (const block of response.content) {
3310	  if (block.type === "thinking") {
3311	    console.log("Thinking:", block.thinking);
3312	  } else if (block.type === "text") {
3313	    console.log("Response:", block.text);
3314	  }
3315	}
3316	```
3317

3319	
3320	## Error Handling
3321	
3322	Use the SDK's typed exception classes - never check error messages with string matching:
3323	
3324	```typescript
3325	import Anthropic from "@anthropic-ai/sdk";
3326	
3327	try {
3328	  const response = await client.messages.create({...});
3329	} catch (error) {
3330	  if (error instanceof Anthropic.BadRequestError) {
3331	    console.error("Bad request:", error.message);
3332	  } else if (error instanceof Anthropic.AuthenticationError) {
3333	    console.error("Invalid API key");
3334	  } else if (error instanceof Anthropic.RateLimitError) {
3335	    console.error("Rate limited - retry later");
3336	  } else if (error instanceof Anthropic.APIError) {
3337	    console.error(`API error ${error.status}:`, error.message);
3338	  }
3339	}
3340	```
3341	
3342	All classes extend `Anthropic.APIError` with a typed `status` field. Check from most specific to least specific. See [shared/error-codes.md](../../shared/error-codes.md) for the full error code reference.
3343	
3344	---
3345	
3346	## Multi-Turn Conversations
3347	
3348	The API is stateless - send the full conversation history each time. Use `Anthropic.MessageParam[]` to type the messages array:
3349	
3350	```typescript
3351	const messages: Anthropic.MessageParam[] = [
3352	  { role: "user", content: "My name is Alice." },
3353	  { role: "assistant", content: "Hello Alice! Nice to meet you." },
3354	  { role: "user", content: "What's my name?" },
3355	];
3356	
3357	const response = await client.messages.create({
3358	  model: "claude-opus-5",
3359	  max_tokens: 16000,
3360	  messages: messages,
3361	});
3362	```
3363	
3364	**Rules:**
3365	
3366	- Consecutive same-role messages are allowed - the API combines them into a single turn
3367	- First message must be `user`
3368	- Use SDK types (`Anthropic.MessageParam`, `Anthropic.Message`, `Anthropic.Tool`, etc.) for all API data structures - don't redefine equivalent interfaces
3369	
3370	---
3371	
3372	### Compaction (long conversations)
3373	
3374	> **Beta, Fable 5, Claude Opus 5, Opus 4.8, Opus 4.7, Opus 4.6, and Sonnet 4.6.** When conversations approach the 200K context window, compaction automatically summarizes earlier context server-side. The API returns a `compaction` block; you must pass it back on subsequent requests - append `response.content`, not just the text.
3375	
3376	```typescript
3377	import Anthropic from "@anthropic-ai/sdk";
3378	
3379	const client = new Anthropic();
3380	const messages: Anthropic.Beta.BetaMessageParam[] = [];
3381	
3382	async function chat(userMessage: string): Promise<string> {
3383	  messages.push({ role: "user", content: userMessage });
3384	
3385	  const response = await client.beta.messages.create({
3386	    betas: ["compact-2026-01-12"],
3387	    model: "claude-opus-5",
3388	    max_tokens: 16000,
3389	    messages,
3390	    context_management: {
3391	      edits: [{ type: "compact_20260112" }],
3392	    },
3393	  });
3394	
3395	  // Append full content - compaction blocks must be preserved
3396	  messages.push({ role: "assistant", content: response.content });
3397	
3398	  const textBlock = response.content.find(
3399	    (b): b is Anthropic.Beta.BetaTextBlock => b.type === "text",
3400	  );
3401	  return textBlock?.text ?? "";
3402	}
3403	
3404	// Compaction triggers automatically when context grows large
3405	console.log(await chat("Help me build a Python web scraper"));
3406	console.log(await chat("Add support for JavaScript-rendered pages"));
3407	console.log(await chat("Now add rate limiting and error handling"));
3408	```
3409	
3410	---
3411	
3412	## Stop Reasons
3413	
3414	The `stop_reason` field in the response indicates why the model stopped generating:
3415	
3416	| Value           | Meaning                                                         |
3417	| --------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------- |
3418	| `end_turn`      | Claude finished its response naturally                          |
3419	| `max_tokens`    | Hit the `max_tokens` limit - increase it or use streaming       |
3420	| `stop_sequence` | Hit a custom stop sequence                                      |
3421	| `tool_use`      | Claude wants to call a tool - execute it and continue           |
3422	| `pause_turn`    | Model paused and can be resumed (agentic flows)                 |
3423	| `refusal`       | Claude refused for safety reasons - check `stop_details`        |
3424	
3425	### Structured Stop Details
3426	
3427	When `stop_reason` is `"refusal"`, the response includes a `stop_details` object with structured information about the refusal:
3428	
3429	```typescript
3430	if (response.stop_reason === "refusal" && response.stop_details) {
3431	  console.log(`Category: ${response.stop_details.category}`); // e.g. "cyber", "bio", "reasoning_extraction", "frontier_llm", or null - see docs for the full set
3432	  console.log(`Explanation: ${response.stop_details.explanation}`);
3433	}
3434	```
3435	
3436	### Refusal Fallbacks (Claude Fable 5) - opt in by default
3437	
3438	Fallbacks are **opt-in**: without them a refused request simply stops. Include the server-side `fallbacks` parameter in `claude-fable-5` code by default - on a policy decline the API re-runs the same request on the fallback model inside the same call. A decline before any output isn't billed (a mid-stream decline bills the streamed partial); the rescue bills at the fallback model's own rates, with cache repricing applied automatically.
3439	
3440	```typescript
3441	const response = await client.beta.messages.create({
3442	  model: "claude-fable-5",
3443	  max_tokens: 16000,
3444	  betas: ["server-side-fallback-2026-06-01"],
3445	  fallbacks: [{ model: "claude-opus-4-8" }],
3446	  messages: [{ role: "user", content: "..." }],
3447	});
3448	
3449	// Switch points: one fallback block per model that ran and declined this turn
3450	for (const block of response.content) {
3451	  if (block.type === "fallback") {
3452	    console.log(`${block.from.model} declined; ${block.to.model} continued`);
3453	  }
3454	}
3455	
3456	// Served-by signal - covers sticky turns, which carry no fallback block.
3457	// Pair with stop_reason: the fallback model can itself refuse.
3458	const fallbackRan = (response.usage.iterations ?? []).some(
3459	  (entry) => entry.type === "fallback_message",
3460	);
3461	if (fallbackRan && response.stop_reason !== "refusal") {
3462	  console.log(`Served by ${response.model}`);
3463	}
3464	```
3465	
3466	A `stop_reason: "refusal"` on the final response means the whole chain refused. The header must be exactly `server-side-fallback-2026-06-01` **for this array form**; the newer `fallbacks: "default"` scalar form uses `server-side-fallback-2026-07-01` instead (see `shared/model-migration.md` -> Migrating to Claude Opus 5 -> New API features), and pairing either header with the other form returns a 400. The parameter is rejected on the Batches API and unavailable on Amazon Bedrock, Vertex AI, and Microsoft Foundry - register the client-side `betaRefusalFallbackMiddleware` on the client there instead. Full semantics (sticky routing, billing, streaming, echoing fallback turns back): `shared/model-migration.md` -> Migrating to Claude Fable 5 -> `refusal` stop reason.
3467

3470	## Cost Optimization Strategies
3471	
3472	### 1. Use Prompt Caching for Repeated Context
3473	
3474	```typescript
3475	// Automatic caching (simplest - caches the last cacheable block)
3476	const response = await client.messages.create({
3477	  model: "claude-opus-5",
3478	  max_tokens: 16000,
3479	  cache_control: { type: "ephemeral" },
3480	  system: largeDocumentText, // e.g., 50KB of context
3481	  messages: [{ role: "user", content: "Summarize the key points" }],
3482	});
3483	
3484	// First request: full cost
3485	// Subsequent requests: ~90% cheaper for cached portion
3486	```
3487	
3488	### 2. Use Token Counting Before Requests
3489	
3490	```typescript
3491	const countResponse = await client.messages.countTokens({
3492	  model: "claude-opus-5",
3493	  messages: messages,
3494	  system: system,
3495	});
3496	
3497	const estimatedInputCost = countResponse.input_tokens * 0.000005; // $5/1M tokens
3498	console.log(`Estimated input cost: $${estimatedInputCost.toFixed(4)}`);
3499	```
3500	</doc>

3600	  ---
3601	  title: Service tiers
3602	  url: https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/api/service-tiers
3603	  description: Different tiers of service allow you to balance availability, performance, and predictable costs based on your application's needs.
3604	  ---
3605	  
3606	  <Warning>
3607	    Priority Tier capacity commitments are no longer available for purchase. Organizations with an existing commitment can continue to use Priority Tier through their contract end date, and this page remains available as a reference for them. If you need guaranteed capacity, [contact sales](https://claude.com/contact-sales).
3608	  </Warning>
3609	  
3610	  Anthropic offers three service tiers:
3611	  
3612	  * **Priority Tier:** Available only to organizations with an existing capacity commitment
3613	  * **Standard:** Default tier for both piloting and scaling everyday use cases
3614	  * **Batch:** Best for asynchronous workflows that can wait or benefit from being outside your normal capacity
3615	  
3616	  ## Standard Tier
3617	  
3618	  The standard tier is the default service tier for all API requests. The API prioritizes these requests alongside all other requests with best-effort availability.
3619	  
3620	  ## Priority Tier
3621	  
3622	  The API prioritizes requests in this tier over all other requests. This prioritization helps minimize ["server overloaded" errors](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/api/errors#http-errors), even during peak times.
3623	  
3624	  For more information, see [Existing Priority Tier commitments](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/api/service-tiers#existing-priority-tier-commitments).
3625	  
3626	  ## How requests get assigned tiers
3627	  
3628	  When handling a request, Anthropic decides to assign a request to Priority Tier in the following scenarios:
3629	  
3630	  * Your organization has sufficient Priority Tier capacity **input** tokens per minute
3631	  * Your organization has sufficient Priority Tier capacity **output** tokens per minute
3632	  
3633	  Anthropic counts usage against Priority Tier capacity as follows:
3634	  
3635	  **Input tokens**
3636	  
3637	  * Cache reads as 0.1 tokens per token read from the cache
3638	  * Cache writes as 1.25 tokens per token written to the cache with a 5 minute TTL
3639	  * Cache writes as 2.00 tokens per token written to the cache with a 1 hour TTL
3640	  * For [US-only inference](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/manage-claude/data-residency) (`inference_geo: "us"`) requests on Claude 4.6 and later models, input tokens are 1.1 tokens per token
3641	  * All other input tokens are 1 token per token
3642	  
3643	  **Output tokens**
3644	  
3645	  * For [US-only inference](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/manage-claude/data-residency) (`inference_geo: "us"`) requests on Claude 4.6 and later models, output tokens are 1.1 tokens per token
3646	  * All other output tokens are 1 token per token
3647	  
3648	  Otherwise, requests proceed at standard tier.
3649	  
3650	  <Note>
3651	    These burndown rates reflect the relative pricing of each token type. For example, US-only inference is priced at 1.1x on Claude 4.6 and later models, so each token consumed with `inference_geo: "us"` draws down 1.1 tokens from your Priority Tier capacity.
3652	  </Note>
3653	  
3654	  <Note>
3655	    Requests assigned Priority Tier pull from both the Priority Tier capacity and the regular rate limits. If servicing the request would exceed the rate limits, the request is declined.
3656	  </Note>
3657	  
3658	  ## Using service tiers
3659	  
3660	  You can control which service tiers can be used for a request by setting the `service_tier` parameter:
3661	  
3662	  <CodeGroup>
3663	    ```bash cURL
3664	    curl https://api.anthropic.com/v1/messages \
3665	      -H "x-api-key: $ANTHROPIC_API_KEY" \
3666	      -H "anthropic-version: 2023-06-01" \
3667	      -H "content-type: application/json" \
3668	      -d '{
3669	        "model": "claude-opus-4-8",
3670	        "max_tokens": 1024,
3671	        "messages": [{"role": "user", "content": "Hello, Claude!"}],
3672	        "service_tier": "auto"
3673	      }'
3674	    ```
3675	  
3676	    ```bash CLI
3677	    ant messages create --transform usage.service_tier --raw-output <<'YAML'
3678	    model: claude-opus-4-8
3679	    max_tokens: 1024
3680	    messages:
3681	      - role: user
3682	        content: Hello, Claude!
3683	    service_tier: auto  # Automatically use Priority Tier when available, fallback to standard
3684	    YAML
3685	    ```
3686	  
3687	    ```python Python
3688	    client = anthropic.Anthropic()
3689	  
3690	    message = client.

3835	  content:
3836	    /**
3837	     * WP-04 — cost engine: prices token buckets at published Anthropic API
3838	     * rates (decision D2) using a `PricingConfig` (`pricing.ts`).
3839	     *
3840	     * Pure functions, no config file dependency: the worker receives the
3841	     * pricing over the protocol. Two entry points matter to callers:
3842	     *  - `priceRequest` prices what actually happened (`RequestRecord`).
3843	     *  - `priceTokens` prices arbitrary buckets, so the simulator (WP-05) can
3844	     *    reprice a request under a counterfactual TTL with the same modifiers.
3845	     *
3846	     * Rate model (pricing page, "Feature-specific pricing"): the `service_tier`
3847	     * and `speed` multipliers scale both the input-side and output rates;
3848	     * cache multipliers then apply to the (scaled) input rate. Missing
3849	     * multiplier keys mean 1.0 — unknown *models* are never guessed (contract
3850	     * degradation policy); unknown *tiers/speeds* are priced at standard.
3851	     */
3852	    
3853	    import type { CostBreakdown, RequestRecord, RequestUsage, UnknownModelReport } from './contract'
3854	    import type { CacheMultipliers, ModelPricing, PricingConfig } from './pricing'
3855	    
3856	    const TOKENS_PER_MTOK = 1_000_000
3857	    
3858	    /** Token buckets a price is computed from — the unit the simulator reprices. */
3859	    export interface TokenBuckets {
3860	      baseInputTokens: number
3861	      cacheReadTokens: number
3862	      cacheWrite5mTokens: number
3863	      cacheWrite1hTokens: number
3864	      outputTokens: number
3865	    }
3866	    
3867	    export interface RateModifiers {
3868	      serviceTier: string
3869	      speed: string
3870	    }
3871	    
3872	    export const ZERO_COST: CostBreakdown = Object.freeze({
3873	      baseInputUsd: 0,
3874	      cacheReadUsd: 0,
3875	      cacheWrite5mUsd: 0,
3876	      cacheWrite1hUsd: 0,
3877	      outputUsd: 0,
3878	      totalUsd: 0,
3879	    })
3880	    
3881	    /** Multiplier lookup with the schema's defaulting rule: missing = 1.0. */
3882	    export function multiplierFor(map: Record<string, number> | undefined, key: string): number {
3883	      if (!map || !Object.hasOwn(map, key)) return 1
3884	      return map[key]
3885	    }
3886	    
3887	    /** Price token buckets for one model under the given modifiers. */
3888	    export function priceTokens(
3889	      buckets: TokenBuckets,
3890	      model: ModelPricing,
3891	      cache: CacheMultipliers,
3892	      modifiers: RateModifiers,
3893	    ): CostBreakdown {
3894	      const scale =
3895	        multiplierFor(model.serviceTierMultipliers, modifiers.serviceTier) *
3896	        multiplierFor(model.speedMultipliers, modifiers.speed)
3897	      const inputRate = model.inputPerMTok * scale
3898	      const outputRate = model.outputPerMTok * scale
3899	      const perToken = (tokens: number, rate: number) => (tokens / TOKENS_PER_MTOK) * rate
3900	    
3901	      const baseInputUsd = perToken(buckets.baseInputTokens, inputRate)
3902	      const cacheReadUsd = perToken(buckets.cacheReadTokens, inputRate * cache.read)
3903	      const cacheWrite5mUsd = perToken(buckets.cacheWrite5mTokens, inputRate * cache.write5m)
3904	      const cacheWrite1hUsd = perToken(buckets.cacheWrite1hTokens, inputRate * cache.write1h)
3905	      const outputUsd = perToken(buckets.outputTokens, outputRate)
3906	      return {
3907	        baseInputUsd,
3908	        cacheReadUsd,
3909	        cacheWrite5mUsd,
3910	        cacheWrite1hUsd,
3911	        outputUsd,
3912	        totalUsd: baseInputUsd + cacheReadUsd + cacheWrite5mUsd + cacheWrite1hUsd + outputUsd,
3913	      }
3914	    }
3915	    
3916	    /**
3917	     * Cache-write tokens the `cache_creation` split does not account for
3918	     * (total exceeds 5m + 1h — e.g. a log written before the split existed).
3919	     */
3920	    export function unattributedWriteTokens(usage: RequestUsage): number {
3921	      return Math.max(
3922	        0,
3923	        usage.cacheCreationInputTokens - usage.cacheCreation5mTokens - usage.cacheCreation1hTokens,
3924	      )
3925	    }
3926	    
3927	    /**
3928	     * Observed usage as token buckets. Unattributed write tokens are priced at
3929	     * the 5m rate — the API's default TTL when none is requested — rather than
3930	     * dropped, so actual cost never understates the bill.
3931	     */
3932	    export function bucketsFromUsage(usage: RequestUsage): TokenBuckets {
3933	      return {
3934	        baseInputTokens: usage.inputTokens,
3935	        cacheReadTokens: usage.cacheReadInputTokens,
3936	        cacheWrite5mTokens: usage.cacheCreation5mTokens + unattributedWriteTokens(usage),
3937	        cacheWrite1hTokens: usage.cacheCreation1hTokens,
3938	        outputTokens: usage.outputTokens,
3939	      }
3940	    }

4141	      rows={}

4161	  def cost(rows, ttl):
4162	      total=0.0; expired=0
4163	      prev=None
4164	      for r in rows:
4165	          pi,po=PRICE.get(r['model'],(5,25))
4166	          gap=(r['ts']-prev).total_seconds() if prev else 1e9
4167	          prev=r['ts']

4169	          read=r['read']; write=r['wtot']

4171	              write+=read; read=0; expired+=1
4172	          total += (r['inp']*pi + read*0.1*pi + write*wmult*pi + r['out']*po)/1e6
4173	      return total,expired

4182	  ## 1. MVP definition
4183	  
4184	  A static web app, deployed to Cloudflare, where a user:
4185	  
4186	  1. Uploads **one Claude Code session log (JSONL)** at a time — or loads a
4187	     bundled sample.
4188	  2. Watches analysis run **entirely in their browser** (Web Worker), with a
4189	     progress indicator and a cancel button.
4190	  3. Gets a verdict: what the session actually cost at published Anthropic API
4191	     rates, what it *would have cost* under the other cache TTL (5m vs 1h), and a
4192	     recommendation.
4193	  4. Sees cache-behavior insights: cache invalidations (expiry gaps, and hard
4194	     resets from model / effort / version changes), warm-read counts, and short
4195	     educational explanations of each.
4196	  5. Can revisit sessions analyzed earlier in the same browser session
4197	     (in-memory history only — nothing persisted).
4198	  
4199	  Plus: clear instructions for finding session logs on macOS and Windows, a
4200	  prominent privacy statement ("analysis is local; this is open source — verify
4201	  it yourself" with a link to the repo), a dedicated **data policy page** (see
4202	  §3a), and a "prices as of" date on every dollar figure.
4203	  
4204	  Two cross-cutting requirements, cheap now and expensive to retrofit:
4205	  
4206	  - **Localization-ready from day one.** MVP ships English only, but no
4207	    user-facing string is hard-coded in a component: all copy lives in locale
4208	    resource files (react-i18next or equivalent — chosen in WP-07), and all
4209	    number, currency, date, and duration formatting goes through the `Intl`
4210	    APIs keyed to the active locale. Adding a second language later must be a
4211	    translation task, not a refactor.
4212	  - **Data policy page**: a first-class page, linked from the footer and the
4213	    upload screen, stating in plain language: analysis runs entirely in the
4214	    browser; the file is never uploaded, stored, or sent anywhere; the strict
4215	    CSP enforces this at the platform level (with a one-line "check devtools"
4216	    pointer); no analytics or tracking in the MVP; the project is open source
4217	    (repo link); and any future optional API-powered analysis will be opt-in
4218	    with an explicit, itemized disclosure o
4219	  ... [80 characters truncated]

4319	  content:
4320	    /**
4321	     * WP-05 — the counterfactual simulator: replays a parsed session under
4322	     * each cache TTL and produces the per-bucket `AnalysisResult` the UI
4323	     * consumes (docs/PLAN.md §3, feasibility doc §6–§7).
4324	     *
4325	     * MODEL. Requests are partitioned into the two buckets the settings govern
4326	     * ("main" = `promptCacheTtl`, "subagent" = `subagentPromptCacheTtl`) and,
4327	     * inside a bucket, into cache threads (each subagent has its own cache).
4328	     * Within a thread, requests are replayed in file order and the cache entry
4329	     * is alive at request r iff the same-thread gap since the previous request
4330	     * START is within the TTL (reads refresh the timer at no cost, so the clock
4331	     * restarts at every request).
4332	     *
4333	     * The scenario at the OBSERVED TTL reproduces the log exactly (the
4334	     * reconciliation property, PLAN §5). A counterfactual only edits the
4335	     * requests where the two TTL windows disagree, all-or-nothing (§7):
4336	     *  - shortening (observed 1h, scenario 5m): where 5m < gap and the log shows
4337	     *    a cache read, that read lapses and becomes a fresh write at the 5m
4338	     *    rate ("expiry");
4339	     *  - lengthening (observed 5m, scenario 1h): where 5m < gap <= 1h and the
4340	     *    log shows no read but a write, the lapsed prefix (bounded by what was
4341	     *    cached after the previous request) would have been read instead.
4342	     * A change of model, effort, or version between consecutive same-thread
4343	     * requests is a hard reset in every scenario (§6.6): the cache is emptied,
4344	     * no expiry is attributed to that gap, and the observed usage stands.
4345	     *
4346	     * MIXED TTLs (contract, frozen): only the user-controllable write share is
4347	     * repriced — the bucket's dominant-TTL write tokens (tie → 1h). The
4348	     * residual split tokens are server-tool writes, kept at their observed TTL
4349	     * in both scenarios as their own expiry class and never simulated for
4350	     * expiry (their reads are not separable from `cache_read_input_tokens`).
4351	     *
4352	     * UNKNOWN MODELS (contract, frozen): excluded from every dollar figure but
4353	     * still replayed so cache state and events stay right; a bucket whose
4354	     * unpriced token share exceeds `UNKNOWN_MODEL_SUPPRESSION_RATIO` gets
4355	     * "no-verdict".
4356	     */
4357	    
4358	    import {
4359	      CACHE_TTL_1H_MS,
4360	      CACHE_TTL_5M_MS,
4361	      UNKNOWN_MODEL_SUPPRESSION_RATIO,
4362	      type AnalysisResult,
4363	      type BucketAnalysis,
4364	      type BucketId,
4365	      type CacheTtl,
4366	      type CostBreakdown,
4367	      type HardResetCause,

4370	      type Recommendation,

4397	    export function bucketOf(request: RequestRecord): BucketId {
4398	      return request.isSidechain ? 'subagent' : 'main'
4399	    }

4401	    /** Requests grouped by thread, file order preserved within each thread. */
4402	    export function groupByThread(requests: readonly RequestRecord[]): Map<string, RequestRecord[]> {
4403	      const threads = new Map<string, RequestRecord[]>()
4404	      for (const request of requests) {
4405	        let list = threads.get(request.threadId)
4406	        if (!list) {
4407	          list = []
4408	          threads.set(request.threadId, list)
4409	        }
4410	        list.push(request)
4411	      }
4412	      return threads
4413	    }

4430	    /**
4431	     * WP-05 — the real `AnalysisEngine`: streams the file through the parser
4432	     * (WP-03), applies the validation verdict, and runs the simulator (WP-05)
4433	     * with the cost engine (WP-04). Replaces the WP-02 stub behind the same
4434	     * interface, so the worker protocol and its tests are unchanged.
4435	     */
4436	    
4437	    import type { AnalysisEngine, EngineOutcome } from './contract'
4438	    import { knownModelIds } from './cost'
4439	    import { parseSession, rejectionReason } from './parser'
4440	    import { analyzeSession } from './simulator'
4441	    
4442	    export function createEngine(): AnalysisEngine {
4443	      return {
4444	        async analyze(input, { onProgress, signal }) {
4445	          const parsed = await parseSession(input.stream, {
4446	            fileName: input.fileName,
4447	            fileSizeBytes: input.fileSizeBytes,
4448	            knownModels: knownModelIds(input.pricing),
4449	            onProgress,
4450	            signal,
4451	          })
4452	          if (signal.aborted) throw new DOMException('aborted', 'AbortError')
4453	          if (parsed.verdict === 'not-a-session-log') {
4454	            const outcome: EngineOutcome = {
4455	              kind: 'rejected',
4456	              verdict: 'not-a-session-log',
4457	              stats: parsed.stats,
4458	              reason: rejectionReason(parsed),
4459	            }
4460	            return outcome
4461	          }
4462	          onProgress({
4463	            phase: 'analyzing',
4464	            bytesProcessed: input.fileSizeBytes,
4465	            totalBytes: input.fileSizeBytes,
4466	            requestsSeen: parsed.requests.length,
4467	          })
4468	          const result = analyzeSession(parsed, input.pricing)
4469	          if (signal.aborted) throw new DOMException('aborted', 'AbortError')
4470	          return { kind: 'analysis', result }
4471	        },
4472	      }
4473	    }

4711	    function fileOf(text: string, name = 'session.jsonl'): WorkerFileInput {
4712	      const bytes = new TextEncoder().encode(text)
4713	      return {
4714	        name,
4715	        size: bytes.byteLength,
4716	        stream: () =>
4717	          new ReadableStream<Uint8Array>({
4718	            start(controller) {
4719	              // Two chunks so the parser crosses a boundary.
4720	              controller.enqueue(bytes.subarray(0, 100))
4721	              controller.enqueue(bytes.subarray(100))
4722	              controller.close()
4723	            },
4724	          }),
4725	      }
4726	    }
4727

4729	      const file = fileOf(text)
4730	      const progress: EngineProgress[] = []
4731	      const outcome = await createEngine().analyze(
4732	        { stream: file.stream(), fileName: file.name, fileSizeBytes: file.size, pricing: PRICING },
4733	        { onProgress: (p) => progress.push(p), signal },
4734	      )
4735	      return { outcome, progress }
4736	    }

4896	    `fixtures/generated/`, git-ignored, ~1s). Also: `rejectionReason(parsed)`
4897	    derives the `EngineOutcome` reason from `stats`, because the contract's
4898	    `ParsedSession` carries no reason field — no contract change; legacy
4899	    sidechain recovery indexes `uuid`/`parentUuid` of assistant rows too
4900	    (metadata only) and stops at the first non-sidechain ancestor; a request's

5067	Base directory for this skill: ~/Code/cache-ttl-analyzer/.claude/skills/publish-transcript
5068	
5069	# Publish a session transcript
5070	
5071	Turns one Claude Code session for this repo into one committed folder holding
5072	both published forms:
5073	
5074	```
5075	transcripts/<NNN-slug>/
5076	  session.jsonl    the redacted session log
5077	  index.html       rendered via simonw/claude-code-transcripts
5078	  page-001.html
5079	```
5080	
5081	**The HTML is rendered from the redacted JSONL, never from the original.**
5082	Redact once at the source and both outputs stay consistent — never redact the
5083	rendered HTML separately.
5084	
5085	`to_text.py` renders a session as plain text for *your* review in step 2. That
5086	rendering is a working file: it is never committed. Only `session.jsonl` and the
5087	rendered HTML go into the repo.
5088	
5089	## Prerequisites
5090	
5091	`claude-code-transcripts` must be on PATH. If `which claude-code-transcripts`
5092	comes up empty, run it via `uvx claude-code-transcripts` instead, or install with
5093	`uv tool install claude-code-transcripts`.
5094	
5095	Scripts live in `.claude/skills/publish-transcript/scripts/`. Below, `$S` means
5096	that directory and `$W` a scratch working directory (use the session scratchpad,
5097	not the repo — intermediate files must never be committed).
5098	
5099	## Step 1 — Pick the session
5100	
5101	```bash
5102	python3 $S/list_sessions.py --limit 20
5103	```
5104	
5105	Show the list and ask the user which session to publish, unless they already
5106	named one (by session id, by "the current session", or by describing its topic
5107	clearly enough to match exactly one row).
5108	
5109	**Publishing the current session:** the JSONL is still being written, so it will
5110	not contain this final turn. That is expected and fine — say so, and offer to
5111	re-run later if they want the tail included. Note that the skill is loaded at
5112	session start, so a session that *created or edited* the skill cannot invoke it
5113	by name; follow these steps directly instead.
5114	
5115	## Step 2 — Stage and render for review
5116	
5117	```bash
5118	mkdir -p $W
5119	cp ~/.claude/projects/<project-slug>/<session-id>.jsonl $W/original.jsonl
5120	python3 $S/to_text.py $W/original.jsonl -o $W/review.txt --max-tool-output 4000
5121	```
5122	
5123	`list_sessions.py` prints the absolute source path — copy it from there rather
5124	than reconstructing it.
5125	
5126	## Step 3 — Find sensitive material
5127	
5128	Two passes, both required. The regex pass catches well-shaped secrets; the
5129	reading pass catches everything shaped like ordinary prose.
5130	
5131	**Pass A — regex triage:**
5132	
5133	```bash
5134	python3 $S/scan_secrets.py $W/original.jsonl
5135	```
5136	
5137	**Pass B — read the transcript.** Read `$W/review.txt` in full. For a long
5138	session, read it in chunks — do not skim, and do not delegate this to a subagent
5139	that only reports back a summary. You are looking for things a regex cannot
5140	match:
5141	
5142	- Credentials, tokens, API keys, passwords, connection strings — including ones
5143	  pasted mid-sentence or shown in command output
5144	- Real names, emails, phone numbers, addresses of anyone (the user included)
5145	- Customer, client, or employer names the user may not want public
5146	- Internal hostnames, private URLs, ticket IDs, Slack/DM links, S3 buckets
5147	- **Names of the user's other projects.** A session that ran `ls ~/.claude/projects`,
5148	  `git remote -v` elsewhere, or any directory listing above the repo will name
5149	  unrelated work. A regex cannot catch this — it is the single most likely real
5150	  leak in an otherwise clean session.
5151	- Unreleased or confidential product details discussed in passing
5152	- `.env` file contents, private keys, or `~/.aws/credentials` echoed by a tool
5153	- Anything in a file the repo's `.gitignore` excludes (this repo ignores
5154	  `.env`, `.env.*`) — if its contents appear in the transcript, redact them
5155	
5156	Judgment calls to raise with the user rather than decide alone:
5157	
5158	- **The user's own name and email.** They are already in the git history of this
5159	  repo, so redacting them is usually pointless — but ask, don't assume.
5160	- **Home directory paths** (`/Users/<name>/...`). Low risk, but they leak a
5161	  username and local layout. Offer to rewrite them to `~/`.
5162	- **Third parties' names.** Default to redacting these; a person who was
5163	  mentioned in a session did not consent to being published.
5164	
5165	## Step 4 — Propose redactions
5166	
5167	Write `$W/redactions.json` — a list of rules, each with a `reason`:
5168	
5169	```json
5170	[
5171	  {"find": "sk-ant-api03-REAL-KEY", "replace": "[REDACTED_API_KEY]", "reason": "Anthropic API key in tool output"},
5172	  {"find": "coworker@example.com", "replace": "[REDACTED_EMAIL]", "reason": "third party PII"},
5173	  {"find": "~", "replace": "~", "reason": "local home path"}
5174	]
5175	```
5176	
5177	Rules are literal by default; add `"regex": true` for a pattern. Use
5178	`[REDACTED_<KIND>]` placeholders so a reader can tell *what* was removed.
5179	
5180	**Redact the shortest distinctive token, not the full prefixed value.** A
5181	transcript contains *truncated* copies of its own content — tool output gets cut
5182	off, scanner context windows clip mid-value, and prose refers to values with an
5183	ellipsis. A rule for `sk-ant-api03-<key>` will sail past `...-api03-<key>` and
5184	`sk-ant-api03-<pre>...`, leaving fragments behind. Add a rule for the bare core
5185	(`<key>`) as well as the full value, and for a multi-part name like
5186	`acme-widget-service` add one for `acme` too.
5187	
5188	**Watch for self-reference.** Once a session discusses its own redaction, the
5189	sensitive strings appear in the discussion — in your grep commands, your
5190	findings summary, and your questions to the user. Re-copying the source JSONL
5191	after that point pulls those in, and each re-copy adds more. Copy the source
5192	once, redact and verify against that snapshot, and stop; do not chase the tail.
5193	
5194	Present the proposed list to the user — grouped by category, with the reason and
5195	match count for each — and get approval before applying. **If you find a live
5196	credential, tell the user plainly that it should be rotated**, since it existed
5197	in plaintext on disk regardless of what gets published.
5198	
5199	If both passes come up clean, say so explicitly and confirm they want to publish
5200	unredacted rather than silently proceeding.
5201	
5202	## Step 5 — Apply and verify
5203	
5204	```bash
5205	python3 $S/apply_redactions.py $W/original.jsonl $W/redactions.json \
5206	    -o $W/redacted.jsonl --verify
5207	python3 $S/scan_secrets.py $W/redacted.jsonl
5208	```
5209	
5210	`--verify` fails if any literal target survived. Investigate a `NO MATCHES`
5211	warning before continuing — it usually means a typo'd rule, which means
5212	something you meant to redact is still in the file.
5213	
5214	The second scan is the real check: re-read its output and confirm every
5215	remaining hit is something you and the user consciously decided to keep.
5216	
5217	## Step 6 — Render the two artifacts
5218	
5219	Name the session `NNN-short-topic`: a zero-padded counter one higher than the
5220	highest folder already in `transcripts/`, plus a kebab-case topic — e.g.
5221	`002-cost-model`.
5222	
5223	`--json` copies the JSONL into the output directory alongside the HTML, naming
5224	it after the input file. Rename the redacted file to `session.jsonl` first so it
5225	lands with the right name, and the whole folder is produced in one command:
5226	
5227	```bash
5228	SLUG=<NNN-short-topic>
5229	mv $W/redacted.jsonl $W/session.jsonl
5230	claude-code-transcripts json $W/session.jsonl -o transcripts/$SLUG --json
5231	```
5232	
5233	Do not pass `--gist` — that uploads to a public Gist, which is a separate
5234	publishing decision the user has to make explicitly.
5235	
5236	Then confirm the redaction survived rendering:
5237	
5238	```bash
5239	python3 $S/scan_secrets.py transcripts/$SLUG/*
5240	```
5241	
5242	## Step 7 — Update the session map
5243	
5244	Add a row to the table in `transcripts/README.md`:
5245	
5246	| Date | Session | Covers | Share link | Notes |
5247	|---|---|---|---|---|
5248	| 2026-08-30 | [001-transcript-skill](001-transcript-skill/) | Built the transcript publishing skill | | 3 emails redacted |
5249	
5250	Keep rows in date order. Put a short, honest note about what was redacted in the
5251	Notes column — that is the reader's signal that gaps are deliberate. Leave the
5252	share link blank unless the user has published one.
5253	
5254	**Ask the user what the session covers — don't just write it yourself.** Propose
5255	a one-line summary drawn from reading the transcript, and ask them to confirm or
5256	replace it. They know why the session mattered; the transcript only shows what
5257	happened in it.
5258	
5259	Aim the summary at *why the work was done*, not the mechanics of what was
5260	typed. Where a session advanced a stated goal of the project, say so — that
5261	connection is the reason the transcript is worth keeping, and it is exactly the
5262	part a reader cannot reconstruct from the log.
5263	
5264	- Weak: "Created four Python scripts and a SKILL.md"
5265	- Better: "Built the transcript publishing skill, so build sessions can be
5266	  published without leaking secrets or PII — a stated goal of the project"
5267	
5268	Use the same confirmed summary for the commit subject in step 8, so the map row
5269	and the git history agree.
5270	
5271	## Step 8 — Commit
5272	
5273	This repo uses [Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/).
5274	Transcript publishing is a `docs` change:
5275	
5276	```bash
5277	git add transcripts/
5278	git commit -m "docs(transcripts): add session log for <topic>"
5279	```
5280	
5281	Guidance:
5282	
5283	- Subject in imperative mood, no trailing period, under ~72 characters.
5284	- If anything was redacted, note it in the body — e.g.
5285	  `Redacted 2 API keys and 3 third-party email addresses before publishing.`
5286	- Commit only under `transcripts/`; leave unrelated working-tree changes alone.
5287	- Do not push unless the user asks. Publishing to a remote is their call, and it
5288	  is the point of no return for anything that slipped through redaction.
5289	
5290	[tool use: Bash]
5291	  command:
5292	    cd ~/Code/cache-ttl-analyzer

2300	        const mostlyGiant = turns.slice(0, 3).map((r) => JSON.stringify(r)).join('\\n') + '\\n' + giant + '\\n'
2301	        const rejected = await parseSession(streamOf(mostlyGiant, 64), {
2302	          fileName: 's.jsonl',

2305	        })

2307	        expect(rejectionReason(rejected)).toBe('malformed-lines-exceed-threshold')
2308	      })
2309
